, equation (13.19.3)]
,

W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z)=\mathrm{e}^{-Z/2}Z^{\kappa}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(Z^{-1}\big)\big),\qquad Z\to\infty,\qquad|\mathrm{arg}(Z)|\leq\frac{3\pi}{2}-\delta

holds for each
\delta>0
. Hence also

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{\kappa-2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(Z^{-1}\big)\big)&(-Z)^{-\kappa}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(Z^{-1}\big)\big)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
Z^{\kappa}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(Z^{-1}\big)\big)&(-Z)^{-\kappa}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(Z^{-1}\big)\big)\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{-Z\sigma_{3}/2},\\
&Z\to\infty,\qquad|\mathrm{arg}(Z)|\leq\frac{3\pi}{2}-\delta.\end{split}

Under the condition given on
\mathrm{arg}(Z)
, we have

(-Z)^{-\kappa}=Z^{-\kappa}\begin{cases}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa},&\text{$\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0$ \ (i.e., $0<\operatorname{Arg}(Z)<\pi$),}\\
\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa},&\text{$\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0$ \ (i.e., $-\pi<\operatorname{Arg}(Z)<0$).}\end{cases}

To calculate
Z^{\pm\kappa}
, we recall
Z=\mathrm{i}x\lambda
and use
[
5
, equation (49)]
:

-\dfrac{\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x)<\mathrm{arg}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}(\lambda)<\dfrac{3\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x),\qquad|\lambda|\to\infty.

Next,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0

\displaystyle\implies 0<\operatorname{Arg}(Z)<\pi\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\implies\!-\frac{\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x)<\mathrm{arg}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}(\lambda)<\frac{\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x),\!

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0

\displaystyle\implies-\pi<\operatorname{Arg}(Z)<0\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\implies\!-\frac{3\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x)<\mathrm{arg}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}(\lambda)-2\pi<-\dfrac{\pi}{2}-\operatorname{Arg}(x)\!

and hence, for any
x\in{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}
such that
|{\operatorname{Arg}}(x)|<\pi
,

Z^{\pm\kappa}=x^{\pm\kappa}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\pm\kappa}\begin{cases}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa/2},&\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0,\\
\mathrm{e}^{\mp 3\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa/2},&\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0.\end{cases}

Therefore, for
\lambda
large such that
\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{W}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbf{D}_{n}\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1})&\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\big)\\
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\end{bmatrix}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\lambda\sigma_{3}/2},

so choosing
\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}
so that (
7.25
) is consistent with (
7.1
) in the sector
\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0
requires that
\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}
is an off-diagonal matrix, namely,

\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\\
-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}&0\end{bmatrix}.

Similarly, for
\lambda
large such that
\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{W}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbf{D}_{n}\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1})&\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\big)\\
\mathrm{e}^{-3\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-1}\big)\end{bmatrix}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\lambda\sigma_{3}/2},

so consistency of (
7.25
) with (
7.1
) in the sector
\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0
requires

\mathbf{K}_{n}^{-}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathrm{e}^{3\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\\
-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}/2}\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}&0\end{bmatrix}.

Some additional useful information can be gleaned by enforcing on

\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
the jump conditions for
|\lambda|>2
. The jump rays are illustrated in the
Z
-plane with their orientations in Figure
9
.

•
\infty
\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0
\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0
<
>
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{-}

Figure 9:
Jump contour for
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
near
\lambda=\infty
.

The Whittaker function
W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z)
can be viewed as an analytic function on the cut plane
|{\operatorname{Arg}}(Z)|<\pi
, and it follows from the connection formula
[
38
, equation (13.14.13)]
that the boundary values on the negative real axis are related by

\displaystyle W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z+\mathrm{i}0)=\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z-\mathrm{i}0)

\displaystyle\hphantom{W_{\kappa,\mu_n}(-Z+\mathrm{i}0)=}{}+\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)}W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z),\qquad Z>0.

(7.26)

Note that the denominators in the second term on the right-hand side of (
7.26
) are finite due to condition (iii) in the definition of generic data; see the beginning of Section
7
. Indeed, it follows from (
6.10
) and (
7.12
) that

e_{1}^{\pm 2}=e_{\infty}^{2}\ \Leftrightarrow\ \frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}\pm\mu_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

On
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{-}
the left (
+
) and right (
-
) boundary values correspond to limits from
{\operatorname{Im}(Z)<0}
and
\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0
, respectively. Therefore, the second column of
\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})
is continuous across
\mathbb{R}_{-}
, and from (
7.26
) (replacing
Z
with
-Z
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{-}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{-1}W_{\kappa-1,\mu_{n}}(Z+\mathrm{i}0)&-Z^{-1}W_{1-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z+\mathrm{i}0)&W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{-1}W_{\kappa-1,\mu_{n}}(Z-\mathrm{i}0)-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}Z^{-1}W_{1-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)}&-Z^{-1}W_{1-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z-\mathrm{i}0)+\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)}&W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{W}_{+}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}&0\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa\big)}&1\end{bmatrix},\qquad Z<0.

Here, on the third line we used the definition (
7.16
) of
\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}
and the factorial identity
\Gamma(\diamond+1)=\diamond\Gamma(\diamond)
. Since
\mathbf{H}(\lambda,x)
is analytic across
\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}_{+}
, it follows that
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)=\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
satisfies the jump condition

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{+}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\big[\mathbf{W}_{-}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}\big]^{-1}\mathbf{W}_{+}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{K}_{n}^{-}

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\big(\mathbf{K}^{+}_{n}\big)^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa_{n}}&0\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)}&1\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\mathbf{K}^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\begin{bmatrix}1&\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}}}{\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix},\qquad\lambda\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Requiring that this matches with the corresponding jump condition in Figure
8
gives the condition

\frac{2\pi\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}}}{\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)}=\frac{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}{e_{1}^{2}e_{\infty}^{4}}.

For
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
the
\pm
boundary values correspond to the limit from
\operatorname{Im}(Z)\gtrless 0
. Therefore, now the first column of
\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})
is continuous across
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and from (
7.26
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{-}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{-1}W_{\kappa-1,\mu_{n}}(Z)&-Z^{-1}W_{1-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z+\mathrm{i}0)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z)&W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z+\mathrm{i}0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\!\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{-1}W_{\kappa-1,\mu_{n}}(Z)\!&\displaystyle\!\!-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}Z^{-1}W_{1-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z\!-\mathrm{i}0)+\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}\alpha_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}Z^{-1}W_{\kappa-1,\mu_{n}}(Z)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)}\!\vskip 2.84526pt\\
W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z)\!&\!\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}W_{-\kappa,\mu_{n}}(-Z\!-\mathrm{i}0)+\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}W_{\kappa,\mu_{n}}(Z)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{W}_{+}(Z;\kappa,\mu_{n})\begin{bmatrix}1&\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa\big)}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
0&\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa}\end{bmatrix},\qquad Z>0.

Again here, the finiteness of the denominators is guaranteed by condition (iii) at the beginning of Section
7
. Since
\mathbf{H}(\lambda,x)
changes sign across
\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}_{-}
, we get that
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)=\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{n}(\lambda,x)
satisfies the jump condition

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{+}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=-\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)[\mathbf{W}_{-}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{K}_{n}^{-}]^{-1}\mathbf{W}_{+}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)(\mathbf{K}^{-}_{n})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}-1&\displaystyle-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\big)}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
0&-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa_{n}}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\begin{bmatrix}-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa_{n}}&0\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\kappa_{n}}\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\big)}&-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa_{n}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\begin{bmatrix}e_{\infty}^{2}&0\vskip 2.84526pt\\
e_{\infty}^{2}s_{2}^{\infty}&e_{\infty}^{-2}\end{bmatrix}=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{-}^{(\infty)}(\lambda)\mathbf{S}_{2}^{\infty}e_{\infty}^{2\sigma_{3}},\qquad\lambda\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}_{-}.

The last two equalities follow by a direct calculation using the definitions of
s_{2}^{\infty}
,
\kappa_{n}
,
e_{1}
, and
e_{\infty}
in (
4.30
), (
7.12
), and (
6.10
), respectively, along with the expression

\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}=\dfrac{2\pi\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\kappa_{n}}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\big)}\cdot\frac{e_{1}^{2}e_{\infty}^{4}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}},

(7.27)

and the classical identity

\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-z\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+z\right)=\dfrac{\pi}{\cos(\pi z)}.

(7.28)

7.2.4
The parametrix
\boldsymbol{\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{n}(\lambda,x)}
in the region
\boldsymbol{|\lambda|<2}

We use the identity
[
38
, equation (13.14.33)]
to express the elements of
\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu)
in terms of the alternative basis of solutions
M_{-\kappa,\pm\mu}(-Z)
of Whittaker’s equation with parameters
(\kappa,\mu)
that form a numerically satisfactory pair in a neighborhood of the origin and that are analytic for
\operatorname{Arg}(-Z)\in(-\pi,\pi)
. Moreover, these functions are the Maclaurin series associated with the regular singular point at
Z=0
, so they have the property that

M_{\kappa,\mu}(-Z)(-Z)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}=1+\mathcal{O}(Z)\qquad\text{as}\quad Z\to 0,

(7.29)

where the power function denotes the principal branch and where the error term represents an analytic function of
Z
vanishing at the origin. To deal with the first column of
\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu)
we also use the corresponding identity
M_{\kappa,\mu}(Z)=\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}M_{-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)
which holds for
\pm\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0
(see also
[
38
, equation (13.14.10)]
).
Using the above identities, and under the condition
2\mu\not\in{\mathbb{Z}}
(which follows from the condition (i) at the beginning of Section
7
in our case), we can write the elements of
\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu)
in the form

\displaystyle W_{11}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=-Z^{-1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+\kappa\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu-\kappa\big)\Gamma\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}-\mu+\kappa\bigr)}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}M_{1-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{W_{11}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=}{}-Z^{-1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+\kappa\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-\kappa\big)\Gamma\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}+\mu+\kappa\bigr)}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}M_{1-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z),\quad\!\pm\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0,

\displaystyle W_{12}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=-Z^{-1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}-\mu+\kappa\bigr)}M_{1-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)-Z^{-1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\bigl({-}\frac{1}{2}+\mu+\kappa\bigr)}M_{1-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z),

\displaystyle W_{21}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu-\kappa\big)}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}M_{-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{W_{21}(Z;\kappa,\mu)= }{}+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-\kappa\big)}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}M_{-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z),\qquad\pm\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0,

and

W_{22}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+\kappa\big)}M_{-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+\kappa\big)}M_{-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z).

These expressions can be usefully combined into a matrix identity:

\mathbf{W}(Z;\kappa,\mu)=\mathbf{M}(Z;\kappa,\mu)\mathbf{G}^{\pm}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\pm\operatorname{Im}(Z)>0,

(7.30)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}(Z;\kappa,\mu)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa+\mu\big)Z^{-1}M_{1-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)&\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa-\mu\big)Z^{-1}M_{1-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
M_{-\kappa,\mu}(-Z)&M_{-\kappa,-\mu}(-Z)\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Arg}(-Z)\in(-\pi,\pi),

(7.31)

and

\mathbf{G}^{\pm}_{\kappa,\mu}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu-\kappa\big)}&\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+\kappa\big)}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\pi(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-\kappa\big)}&\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+\kappa\big)}\end{bmatrix}.

To define the parametrix
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
for
|\lambda|<2
, we first introduce a constant matrix by

\mathbf{J}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{G}_{\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}^{+}\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\infty}\mathbf{E}^{\infty}=\mathbf{G}_{\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}^{-}\mathbf{K}_{n}^{-}\mathbf{E}^{\infty}.

(7.32)

The equality of these two expressions can be seen as follows. First, combining (
7.25
) and (
7.30
), and using the fact that the matrix
\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa,\mu_{n})
is analytic in a neighborhood of
\lambda=2\mathrm{i}
, the jump condition for
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
across the positive imaginary axis for
|\lambda|>2
shown in Figure
8
implies the identity
\mathbf{G}_{\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}^{+}\mathbf{K}_{n}^{+}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\infty}=\mathbf{G}_{\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}^{-}\mathbf{K}_{n}^{-}
, which yields the desired equality.

Then, we set

\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{J}_{n},\qquad\text{for}\quad|\lambda|<2.

(7.33)

It is straightforward to then check that, regardless of the choice of
\mu_{n}
,
the matrix
\mathbf{J}_{n}
defined by (
7.32
) is diagonal. Comparing (
7.25
) and (
7.33
) shows that the jump conditions for
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
across the arcs of the circle
|\lambda|=2
shown in Figure
8
are satisfied. Using (
7.29
) then proves that
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
satisfies the simple jump condition across the negative imaginary axis with
{|\lambda|<2}
shown in Figure
8
and that an expansion of the form shown in (
7.2
) holds.
To check that the matrix
\mathbf{J}_{n}
is diagonal and arrive at its final form below, we use the identity (
7.28
) to get

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\dfrac{1}{2}+\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\right)\Gamma\left(\dfrac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\right)=\dfrac{\pi}{\cos\left(\pi(\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n})\right)}=\dfrac{2\pi\mathrm{i}e_{1}e_{\infty}}{e_{1}^{2}-e_{\infty}^{2}},

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\dfrac{1}{2}+\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\right)\Gamma\left(\dfrac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\right)=\dfrac{\pi}{\cos\left(\pi(\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n})\right)}=\dfrac{2\pi\mathrm{i}e_{1}e_{\infty}}{1-e_{1}^{2}e_{\infty}^{2}}.

(7.34)

The result is that the diagonal matrix
\mathbf{J}_{n}
from (
7.32
) is given by

\mathbf{J}_{n}=\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{{\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}e_{1}e_{\infty}^{7/2}\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\big)}&0\\
0&\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{{\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}\big(e_{1}^{4}-1\big)e_{\infty}^{3/2}\Gamma(2\mu_{n})}{e_{1}\big(e_{1}^{2}-e_{\infty}^{2}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\big)}\end{bmatrix}.

(7.35)

7.3
Parametrix for
\boldsymbol{{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)}
near
\boldsymbol{\lambda=0}

By definition, the parametrix
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
satisfies the following Riemann–Hilbert problem.

Riemann–Hilbert Problem 7.3
.

Fix generic monodromy parameters
(e_{1},e_{2})
determining the Stokes and connection matrices,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
x>0
. We seek a
2\times 2
matrix function
\lambda\mapsto\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
satisfying:

•

Analyticity:
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
is analytic in
{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\Gamma^{(0)}
, where
\Gamma^{(0)}=\big\{|\lambda|=\frac{1}{2}\big\}\cup\big(\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}\cap\big\{\operatorname{Im}\lambda<\frac{1}{2}\big\}\big)
is the jump contour shown in Figure
10
.

•

Jump condition:
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
has continuous boundary values on
\Gamma^{(0)}\setminus\{0\}
from each component of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{(0)}
, which satisfy

\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n,+}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)=\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n,-}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)\mathbf{J}_{\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}}(\lambda),

where
\mathbf{J}_{\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}}(\lambda)
is as shown in Figure
10
and where the
+

(
resp.,
-)
subscript denotes a boundary value taken from the left
(
resp., right
)
of an arc of
\Gamma^{(0)}
.

•

Normalization:
\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
satisfies the asymptotic conditions

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)=\mathcal{O}(1)\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}{}}\qquad\text{as}\quad\lambda\to\infty,

(7.36)

where
\mathcal{O}(1)
refers to a function analytic and bounded in a neighborhood of
\lambda=\infty
and

\breve{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)=(\mathbb{I}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda))\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1}\sigma_{3}/2}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{(n+\Theta_{0})\sigma_{3}/2}\qquad\text{as}\quad\lambda\to 0.

(7.37)

Figure 10:
The contour
\Gamma^{(0)}
, which includes the circle
|\lambda|=\frac{1}{2}
.

We can write down the unique solution
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
explicitly in terms of the parametrix
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
obtained in Section
7.2
, but taken with the index
1-n
instead of
n
and
\Theta_{\infty}
replaced by
\Theta_{0}
. If we indicate the dependence of
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
and
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)
on
\Theta_{\infty}
and
\Theta_{0}
respectively with the notation
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)=\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty})
and
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x)=\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})
,
then we have the following.

Proposition 7.4
.

Fix
\Theta_{\infty},\Theta_{0}\in{\mathbb{C}}
and generic monodromy parameters
(e_{1},e_{2})
. Then

\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(0)}_{n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})=\begin{cases}e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{-n}\bigl(-\lambda^{-1},x,\Theta_{0}\bigr)e_{0}^{-2\sigma_{3}}\quad,&|\lambda|<\frac{1}{2},\\
e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{-n}\bigl(-\lambda^{-1},x,\Theta_{0}\bigr)\begin{bmatrix}0&\beta_{n}\\
-\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix},&|\lambda|>\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(7.38)

where

\beta_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\dfrac{1-e_{1}^{4}}{e_{1}^{2}\big(1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}\big)}.

(7.39)

Proof.

The mapping
\lambda\mapsto-\lambda^{-1}
takes the contour
\Gamma^{(0)}
onto the contour
\Gamma^{(\infty)}
up to the reversal of orientation of certain arcs, and swaps the circles centered at the origin of radii
\frac{1}{2}
and
2
. Therefore, the domain of analyticity of
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(0)}_{n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})
is as desired.

Under the map
n\mapsto-n
,
\Theta_{\infty}\mapsto\Theta_{0}
, the exponentials defined in (
6.10
) satisfy
e_{0}^{2}\mapsto e_{\infty}^{2}
, whereas
\mu_{n}=\mu_{-n}
since this quantity depends only on the parity of
n
. This implies that the Stokes matrices defined in (
4.11
)–(
5.15
) satisfy the corresponding identities

\mathbf{S}^{\infty}_{1}\mapsto e_{0}^{-2\sigma_{3}}\big(\mathbf{S}^{0}_{1}\big)^{-1}e_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{S}^{\infty}_{{2}}e_{\infty}^{2\sigma_{3}}\mapsto e_{0}^{-2\sigma_{3}}\big(\mathbf{S}^{0}_{2}e_{0}^{-2\sigma_{3}}\big)^{-1}e_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}}.

Comparing Figures
8
and
10
then shows that the function defined by (
7.38
) satisfied the required jump conditions across the imaginary axis for
|\lambda|<\frac{1}{2}
. Likewise, the jump condition on the negative imaginary axis for
|\lambda|>\frac{1}{2}
is easily verified due to the identity valid for any
a\neq 0
:

e_{1}^{-2\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&a\\
-a^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0&a\\
-a^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}e_{1}^{2\sigma_{3}}=\mathbf{0}.

Finally, the fact that
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(0)}_{n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})
defined by (
7.38
) satisfies the required jump conditions across the circle
|\lambda|=\frac{1}{2}
follows from the corresponding jump conditions for
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{1-n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})
for
|\lambda|=2
and the identities

(\mathbf{E}^{\infty})^{-1}\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}0&\beta_{n}\\
-\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\big(\mathbf{S}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{E}^{0}\big)^{-1}e_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}},\qquad(\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\infty}\mathbf{E}^{\infty})^{-1}\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}0&\beta_{n}\\
-\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\big(\mathbf{E}^{0}\big)^{-1}e_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}}

among the matrices
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}
,
\mathbf{E}^{0}
defined in (
4.31
)–(
4.32
), which hold for the value of
\beta_{n}
indicated in (
7.39
).

It only remains to verify the asymptotics in (
7.36
)–(
7.37
). However, these follow from the corresponding formulæ in (
7.1
)–(
7.2
) with the help of the identity

\lambda^{p}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi p}\zeta^{-p}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}},\qquad\zeta\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=-\lambda^{-1},

which holds for all
\lambda
not on the negative imaginary axis.

Since the matrix function
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})
defined by (
7.38
)–(
7.39
) satisfies all the required Riemann–Hilbert conditions, and there is at most one solution of those conditions, as is easily confirmed by a Liouville argument, the proof is finished.
∎

7.4
An equivalent Riemann–Hilbert problem on the unit circle

The parametrix for
\mathbf{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)
is by definition the following matrix function:

\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}(\lambda,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{cases}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty}),&|\lambda|>1,\\
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0}),&|\lambda|<1.\end{cases}

This matrix function satisfies exactly the same jump conditions in the domains
|\lambda|>1
and
{|\lambda|<1}
as does
\mathbf{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)
itself, and it is also consistent with the asymptotics given in (
6.3
)–(
6.4
) (note that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}(\lambda,x)=\mathbf{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)
for
|\lambda|
sufficiently large or small). The parametrix has unit determinant, so the matrix quotient

\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}(\lambda,x)^{-1}

is an analytic function of
\lambda
except possibly on the jump contour
\Gamma
shown in Figure
6
and on the unit circle, where there is a discontinuity in the definition of
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}(\lambda,x)
. However, since the jumps of
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}(\lambda,x)
and
\mathbf{\Phi}_{n}(\lambda,x)
agree on
\Gamma
, a Morera argument shows that
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
is actually analytic both for
|\lambda|>1
and for
0<|\lambda|<1
. The asymptotic behavior of the factors in
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
as
\lambda\to 0
then shows that any singularity of
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
at the origin
\lambda=0
is removable, and the asymptotic behavior of the same factors in the limit
\lambda\to\infty
shows that
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)\to\mathbb{I}
as
\lambda\to\infty
.

\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
is therefore characterized by its jump condition across the unit circle
|\lambda|=1
. Taking counterclockwise orientation for the circle, the jump condition for
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
reads

\mathbf{Q}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)=\mathbf{Q}_{n,-}(\lambda,x)\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty})e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})^{-1},\qquad|\lambda|=1.

Using Proposition
7.4
, the jump matrix can be written as

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty})e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(0)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{0})^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad=\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{n}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty})e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{-n}(-\lambda^{-1},x,\Theta_{0})^{-1}e_{0}^{-3\sigma_{3}},\qquad|\lambda|=1.

(7.40)

We summarize by writing the Riemann–Hilbert problem for
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
.

Riemann–Hilbert Problem 7.5
.

Fix generic monodromy parameters
(e_{1},e_{2})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
x\in\mathbb{C}
. Seek a
2\times 2
matrix function
\lambda\mapsto\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
with the following properties:

•

Analyticity:
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
is an analytic function of
\lambda
for
|\lambda|\neq 1
.

•

Jump condition:

\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
takes analytic boundary values on the unit circle from the interior and exterior, denoted
\mathbf{Q}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)
and
\mathbf{Q}_{n,-}(\lambda,x)
for
|\lambda|=1
respectively, and they are related by

\mathbf{Q}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)=\mathbf{Q}_{n,-}(\lambda,x)\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x,\Theta_{\infty})e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}^{(\infty)}_{-n}\bigl(-\lambda^{-1},x,\Theta_{0}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}^{-3\sigma_{3}}.

•

Normalization:

\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)\to\mathbb{I}
as
\lambda\to\infty
.

Henceforth, to avoid the notation becoming unwieldy, we understand that all quantities appearing with subscript
n
are evaluated at parameter
\Theta_{\infty}
while quantities appearing with subscript
-n
are evaluated at parameter
\Theta_{0}
.

7.5
The limit
\boldsymbol{n\to+\infty}

Having succeeded in removing the problematic jump conditions along rays emanating from
0
,
\infty
in the
\lambda
plane by defining
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
, we would next like to consider the limiting behavior of this problem as
n\to+\infty
with
x=z/n
and
z
fixed. It is convenient to first renormalize
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
, essentially by a transformation that diagonalizes the coefficient
\mu_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}(x)\sigma_{3}\mathbf{B}_{n}(x)^{-1}
of
\lambda^{-1}
in the matrix of the Lax equation (
7.5
).
In other words, in the jump condition for
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
we prefer to replace
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
with a suitable left-diagonal multiple of
\mathbf{B}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
. Observe that the coefficient
\mathbf{B}_{n}(x)
is determined up to right-multiplication by a diagonal matrix by
(
7.6
), in which the second row of the matrix on the right-hand side is
(c_{n}(x),-a_{n})=\big(\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}},\mu_{n}^{-1}\big(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\big)\big)
, where we used (
7.12
) and (
7.21
). Indeed, the first column
\mathbf{b}_{n}^{(1)}(x)
satisfies
\big(\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}},\mu_{n}^{-1}\big(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\big)-1\big)\mathbf{b}_{n}^{(1)}(x)=0
while the second column
\mathbf{b}_{n}^{(2)}(x)
satisfies
\big(\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}},\mu_{n}^{-1}\big(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\big)+1\big)\mathbf{b}_{n}^{(2)}(x)=0
. By selecting specific constant factors for each column, we obtain a matrix
\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)
differing from
\mathbf{B}_{n}(x)
by right-multiplication by a diagonal matrix, and given explicitly by

\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}&\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\\
\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}}&\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}}\end{bmatrix},

in which the dependence on the index
n
enters via (
7.12
) and (
7.27
). Then to get the desired modification of the jump matrix we set

\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)\mathbf{P}_{n}(x),&|\lambda|>1,\\
\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{P}_{-n}(x)d_{n}(x),&|\lambda|<1,\end{cases}

where
d_{n}(x)
is a scalar satisfying

d_{n}(x)^{2}=\frac{\det(\mathbf{P}_{n}(x))}{\det(\mathbf{P}_{-n}(x))}.

(7.41)

Then,
\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)
solves the following
Riemann–Hilbert problem.

Riemann–Hilbert Problem 7.6
.

Fix generic monodromy parameters
(e_{1},e_{2})
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
x\in\mathbb{C}
. Seek a
2\times 2
matrix function
\lambda\mapsto\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)
with the following properties:

•

Analyticity:
\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)
is an analytic function of
\lambda
for
|\lambda|\neq 1
.

•

Jump condition:

\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)
takes analytic boundary values on the unit circle from the interior and exterior, denoted
\mathbf{R}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)
and
\mathbf{R}_{n,-}(\lambda,x)
for
|\lambda|=1
respectively, and they are related by

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)=\mathbf{R}_{n,-}(\lambda,x)\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{R}_{n,+}(\lambda,x)=}{}\times\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{-n}^{(\infty)}\bigl(-\lambda^{-1},x\bigr)^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{-n}(x)d_{n}(x)

(7.42)

•

Normalization:

\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)\to\mathbb{I}
as
\lambda\to\infty
.

The matrices
\mathbf{\Xi}_{n}^{(6)}(x)
and
\mathbf{\Delta}_{n}^{(6)}(x)
defined in (
6.5
) and (
6.4
), respectively, can be expressed in terms of
\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Xi}_{n}^{(6)}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{n}(x)+\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)\big[\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda(\mathbf{R}_{n}(\lambda,x)-\mathbb{I})\big]\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}x\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{n}^{(6)}(x)=\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)\mathbf{R}_{n}(0,x)\mathbf{P}_{-n}(x)^{-1}d_{n}(x)^{-1}e_{0}^{-3\sigma_{3}}.

(7.43)

Here,
\mathbf{A}_{n}(x)
is the matrix coefficient defined in (
7.1
).

We now show that the jump matrix in (
7.42
) has explicit limits as
n\to+\infty
along even or odd subsequences, with the convergence being uniform for
|\lambda|=1
and bounded
z
where
x=z/n
. To this end, we compute the asymptotic behavior of
\mathbf{P}_{n}\big(n^{-1}z\big)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)
assuming that
|\lambda|=1
. The relevant formula for
\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)
in this setting is (
7.33
).
When
|\lambda|=1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}}}\begin{bmatrix}\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}}&-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-\mu_{n}\gamma_{n}x^{1-2\kappa_{n}}&\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{D}_{n}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{J}_{n}.

Using (
7.24
), we see that

\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)=\frac{1}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{i}&-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-\mathrm{i}&\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}x^{\sigma_{3}/2}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{J}_{n}.

Now, for
x>0
, the principal branch power
(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{\sigma_{3}/2}
has the same domain of analyticity as
\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}
, and these two analytic functions are related by the identity

\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}=x^{-\sigma_{3}/2}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{\sigma_{3}/2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{i}&-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\\
-\mathrm{i}&\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{J}_{n}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}-1&-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\\
1&\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}-\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}(-Z)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(Z;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{J}_{n},\qquad Z=\mathrm{i}x\lambda.

(7.44)

Now using (
7.31
), we have

\displaystyle(-Z)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(Z;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\quad=\begin{bmatrix}\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\bigr)(-Z)^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{1-\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}(-Z)&\bigl(-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}+\kappa_{n}\bigr)(-Z)^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{1-\kappa_{n},-\mu_{n}}(-Z)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
(-Z)^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{-\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}}(-Z)&(-Z)^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{-\kappa_{n},-\mu_{n}}(-Z)\end{bmatrix}.

The diagonal elements in (
7.44
) can be simplified using the identity (see
[
38
, equation (13.15.3)]
)

\big(\kappa-\mu-\tfrac{1}{2}\big)M_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2},\mu+\frac{1}{2}}(\diamond)+(1+2\mu)\diamond^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\kappa,\mu}(\diamond)-\big(\kappa+\mu+\tfrac{1}{2}\big)M_{\kappa+\frac{1}{2},\mu+\frac{1}{2}}(\diamond)=0

replacing
\kappa\to\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}
and
\mu\to\mu_{n}-\frac{1}{2}
for the
(1,1)
entry and
\mu\to-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}
for the
(2,2)
entry,
and the off-diagonal elements can be simplified using the identity (see
[
38
, equation (13.15.4)]
)

2\mu M_{\kappa-\frac{1}{2},\mu-\frac{1}{2}}(\diamond)-2\mu M_{\kappa+\frac{1}{2},\mu-\frac{1}{2}}(\diamond)-\diamond^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\kappa,\mu}(\diamond)=0

replacing
\kappa\to\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}
and
\mu\to\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}
for the
(1,2)
entry and
\mu\to\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}
for the
(2,1)
entry. The result is that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\begin{bmatrix}2\mu_{n}M_{\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n},\mu_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}(-Z)&\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}{1-2\mu_{n}}\right)M_{\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n},\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}}(-Z)\\
\left(\dfrac{\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}}{1+2\mu_{n}}\right)M_{\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n},\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}}(-Z)&2\mu_{n}M_{\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n},-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}}(-Z)\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}_{n},

\displaystyle Z=\mathrm{i}x\lambda.

(7.45)

We will need the following result for the large
n
limit of Whittaker functions appearing here, cf.
[
38
, equation (13.21.1)]
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Assume that
\mu
is fixed with
2\mu\neq-1,-2,-3,\dots
, and let
f(\zeta;\mu)
denote the entire function

f(\zeta;\mu)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\zeta)^{s}}{\Gamma(1+2\mu+s)s!}.

(7.46)

Then the asymptotic formula

M_{\kappa,\mu}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)=\Gamma(1+2\mu)\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)^{\mu+1/2}\big[f(\zeta;\mu)+\mathcal{O}\big(\kappa^{-2}\big)\big]

holds uniformly in the limit
\kappa\to\infty
in any
(
possibly complex
)
direction under the assumption
{\zeta=\mathcal{O}(1)}
.

Proof.

We start from the formula
[
38
, equation (13.14.6)]
which holds under the indicated condition on
\mu
:

M_{\kappa,\mu}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)=\Gamma(1+2\mu)\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)^{\mu+1/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\zeta/(2\kappa)}\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\zeta)^{s}}{\Gamma(1+2\mu+s)s!}\prod_{j=0}^{s-1}\left(1-\frac{\mu+1/2+j}{\kappa}\right).

Clearly,
\mathrm{e}^{-\zeta/(2\kappa)}=1-\zeta/(2\kappa)+\mathcal{O}\big(\kappa^{-2}\big)
as
\kappa\to\infty
for
\zeta=\mathcal{O}(1)
, and the product in the summand has the expansion

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0}^{s-1}\left(1-\frac{\mu+1/2+j}{\kappa}\right)=1-\frac{1}{\kappa}\bigg[\left(\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right)s+\frac{1}{2}s(s-1)\bigg]+\mathcal{O}\big(\kappa^{-2}s^{s}\big),\qquad\kappa\to\infty\!\!\!

(7.47)

uniformly for all indices
s
.
This follows from the Fredholm expansion formula

\prod_{j=0}^{s-1}(1+r_{j})=1+\sum_{j=0}^{s-1}r_{j}+\sum_{k=2}^{s}\mathop{\sum_{S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{s}}}_{|S|=k}\prod_{l\in S}r_{l}

and the estimate

\Biggl|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{s}\\
|S|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{l\in S}r_{l}\Biggr|\leq\binom{s}{k}R_{s}^{k},\qquad R_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max_{0\leq l\leq s-1}\{|r_{l}|\}.

Indeed, with
r_{j}=-\kappa^{-1}(\mu+\frac{1}{2}+j)
, we have

\sum_{j=0}^{s-1}r_{j}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{j=0}^{s-1}\left(\mu+\frac{1}{2}+j\right)=-\frac{1}{\kappa}\left[\left(\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right)s+\frac{1}{2}s(s-1)\right],

and
R_{s}\leq|\kappa|^{-1}\big(\big|\mu+\frac{1}{2}\big|+(s-1)\big)
. Therefore,
R_{s}^{k}\leq|\kappa|^{-2}\big(\big|\mu+\frac{1}{2}\big|+(s-1)\big)^{k}
holds for all
k\geq 2
whenever
|\kappa|\geq 1
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{j=0}^{s-1}\left(1-\frac{\mu+1/2+j}{\kappa}\right)-1+\right.\left.\frac{1}{\kappa}\left[\left(\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right)s+\frac{1}{2}s(s-1)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\Biggl|\sum_{k=2}^{s}\mathop{\sum_{S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{s}}}_{|S|=k}\prod_{l\in S}r_{l}\Biggr|\leq\frac{1}{|\kappa|^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{s}\binom{s}{k}\left(\left|\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right|+(s-1)\right)^{k}1^{s-k}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{|\kappa|^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{s}\binom{s}{k}\left(\left|\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right|+(s-1)\right)^{k}1^{s-k}=\frac{\big(\big|\mu+\frac{1}{2}\big|+s\big)^{s}}{|\kappa|^{2}},

which proves (
7.47
).
Since the series

\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\zeta)^{s}s^{s}}{\Gamma(1+2\mu_{n}+s)s!}

converges uniformly for
|\zeta|
bounded, it follows that

M_{\kappa,\mu}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)=\Gamma(1+2\mu)\left(\frac{\zeta}{\kappa}\right)^{\mu+1/2}\left[f(\zeta;\mu)-\frac{1}{\kappa}g(\zeta;\mu)+\mathcal{O}\big(\kappa^{-2}\big)\right]

holds as
\kappa\to\infty
in
\mathbb{C}
with
\zeta=\mathcal{O}(1)
, where

g(\zeta;\mu)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{2}\zeta f(\zeta;\mu)+\left(\mu+\frac{1}{2}\right)\zeta f^{\prime}(\zeta;\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(\zeta;\mu).

Now using the series (
7.46
) one checks that for all indicated values of
\mu
,
g(\zeta;\mu)
vanishes identically, so the proof is complete.
∎

The series in (
7.46
) defines an entire function of
\zeta
related to Bessel functions (see
[
38
, Chapter 10]
) in the following way:

f(\zeta,\mu)=\zeta^{-{\mu}{}}J_{2\mu}\big(2\sqrt{\zeta}\big).

(7.48)

We apply Lemma
7.7
to (
7.45
) by taking
\zeta=-\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}\big)Z=-\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}\big)\mathrm{i}x\lambda
. If
x=z/n
and
z=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then using (
7.12
), we see that
\zeta=-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}z\lambda+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)
holds for
|\lambda|=1
. So, (
7.45
) becomes the statement that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{n}^{(\infty)}(\lambda,x)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho_{\infty}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})&-\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&-\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\times\left(\dfrac{n}{2}\right)^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{J}_{n}

with

\rho_{\infty}=\rho_{\infty}(\lambda,z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(-2\mathrm{i}z\lambda)^{1/2}\qquad\text{(principal branch)}

(7.49)

holds in the limit
n\to\infty
with
x=n^{-1}z
uniformly for
z=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
|\lambda|=1
. Similarly, to study the parametrix near 0, it will be convenient to rewrite formula (
7.48
) in terms of modified Bessel functions:

f(\zeta,\mu)=(-\zeta)^{-\mu}I_{2\mu}\big(2\sqrt{-\zeta}\big).

Replacing
n
with
-n
,
\Theta_{\infty}
with
\Theta_{0}
, and
\lambda
with
-\lambda^{-1}
and recalling that
\mu_{-n}=\mu_{n}
, gives in the same limit,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{-n}(x)^{-1}\breve{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{-n}^{(\infty)}\bigl(-\lambda^{-1},x\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi/4}}{2\mu_{n}^{2}\gamma_{-n}}x^{\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}{\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)}{}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})&{\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})}{}I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-{\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)}{}I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})&-{\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})}{}I_{-2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\times\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{J}_{-n}

with

\rho_{0}=\rho_{0}(\lambda,z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\big(2\mathrm{i}z\lambda^{-1}\big)^{1/2}\qquad\text{(principal branch)}.

(7.50)

The jump matrix in (
7.42
) therefore reads

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{n,-}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{n,+}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{\infty}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)&-\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)&-\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{}\times{}\frac{\gamma_{-n}}{\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{-n}}d_{n}(x)\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{J}_{n}e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}_{-n}^{-1}\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\frac{2}{\rho_{0}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{}\times\begin{bmatrix}{\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)}{}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)&{\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})}{}I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-{\Gamma(2\mu_{n}+1)}{}I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)&-{\Gamma(1-2\mu_{n})}{}I_{-2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\end{bmatrix}^{-1}.

(7.51)

Expanding (
7.41
) for large
n>0
gives

\displaystyle d_{n}(x)^{2}=\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\gamma_{-n}}x^{2\kappa_{-n}-2\kappa_{n}}=\dfrac{e_{\infty}^{5}}{e_{0}^{5}}\dfrac{e_{0}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n}-\mu_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n}+\mu_{n}\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\big)}x^{2\kappa_{-n}-2\kappa_{n}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{d_n(x)^2}{}=\dfrac{e_{\infty}^{5}e_{1}^{2}}{e_{0}^{3}\big(1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}\big)\big(e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{d_n(x)^2=}{}\times\dfrac{4\pi^{2}x^{2\kappa_{-n}-2\kappa_{n}}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\kappa_{-n}-\mu_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\kappa_{-n}+\mu_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{d_n(x)^2}{}=4\dfrac{e_{\infty}^{4}}{e_{0}^{4}}\dfrac{e_{0}e_{\infty}e_{1}^{2}}{\big(1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}\big)\big(e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}\big)}x^{2\kappa_{-n}-2\kappa_{n}}n^{-2n-\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{\infty}}\mathrm{e}^{2n}2^{2n+\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{\infty}-2}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big),

\displaystyle n\to+\infty.

We now properly define
d_{n}(x)
for large
n
by selecting a definite value for the square root of

\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}

(7.52)

after which
d_{n}(x)
has the asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle d_{n}(x)=\dfrac{e_{1}e_{\infty}^{5/2}}{e_{0}^{3/2}}\dfrac{1}{\big(1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}\big)}\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}x^{\kappa_{-n}-\kappa_{n}}n^{-n+\frac{1}{2}(-\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{\infty})}\mathrm{e}^{n}2^{n+\frac{1}{2}(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{\infty})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{d_n(x) =}{}\times\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big),\qquad n\to+\infty.

Then, by definition, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\gamma_{-n}}{\gamma_{n}}d_{n}(x)x^{\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{-n}}={}

\displaystyle\dfrac{e_{0}^{3/2}}{e_{1}e_{\infty}^{5/2}}{\big(1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}\big)}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{1}^{2}e_{0}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}\right)^{-1}n^{n-\frac{1}{2}(-\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{\infty})}\mathrm{e}^{-n}

\displaystyle\times 2^{-n-\frac{1}{2}(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{\infty})}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big),\qquad n\to+\infty.

Furthermore, using identities (
7.28
), (
7.34
), and Stirling’s formula yields

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{2}{n}\right)^{2\mu_{n}}\beta_{n}\frac{J_{n,11}}{J_{-n,22}}=-\left(\frac{2}{n}\right)^{2\mu_{n}}\frac{e_{\infty}^{7/2}\big(e_{1}^{2}-e_{0}^{2}\big)\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n}+\mu_{n}\big)}{e_{0}^{3/2}\big(e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Gamma(2\mu_{n})\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}-\mu_{n}\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{-\left(\frac{2}{n}\right)^{2\mu_n}\beta_n\frac{J_{n,11}}{J_{-n,22}}}{}=\frac{e_{\infty}^{7/2}}{e_{0}^{3/2}}\dfrac{\mathrm{i}e_{0}e_{1}}{\big(1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}\big)}\dfrac{\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})}{\Gamma(2\mu_{n})}n^{-n+\frac{1}{2}(-\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{\infty})}\mathrm{e}^{n}

\displaystyle\hphantom{-\left(\frac{2}{n}\right)^{2\mu_n}\beta_n\frac{J_{n,11}}{J_{-n,22}}=}{}\times 2^{n+\frac{1}{2}(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{\infty})}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big),\qquad n\to+\infty,

and similarly,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{2\mu_{n}}\frac{J_{n,22}}{\beta_{n}J_{-n,11}}=\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{2\mu_{n}}\frac{e_{\infty}^{3/2}\big(e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Gamma(2\mu_{n})\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n}-\mu_{n}\big)}{e_{0}^{7/2}\big(e_{1}^{2}-e_{\infty}^{2}\big)\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n}+\mu_{n}\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{2\mu_n}\frac{J_{n,22}}{\beta_{n}J_{-n,11}}}{}=\frac{e_{\infty}^{3/2}}{e_{0}^{7/2}}\dfrac{\mathrm{i}e_{0}e_{1}}{\big(e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}\big)}\dfrac{\Gamma(2\mu_{n})}{\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})}n^{-n+\frac{1}{2}(-\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{\infty})}\mathrm{e}^{n}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{2\mu_n}\frac{J_{n,22}}{\beta_{n}J_{-n,11}}=}{}\times 2^{n+\frac{1}{2}(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{\infty})}\big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big),\qquad n\to+\infty.

Therefore, the central factor on the right-hand side of (
7.51
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{-n}}{\gamma_{n}}x^{\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{-n}}d_{n}(x)\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{J}_{n}e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}_{-n}^{-1}\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}0&-\dfrac{e_{0}e_{2}e_{\infty}}{\mathrm{i}}\dfrac{\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})}{\Gamma(2\mu_{n})}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}\right)^{-1}\\
\dfrac{\mathrm{i}}{e_{0}e_{2}e_{\infty}}\dfrac{\Gamma(2\mu_{n})}{\Gamma(-2\mu_{n})}\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}&0\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big).

The leading term is independent of
n\pmod{2}
and has unit determinant. This proves the following.

Proposition 7.8
.

Define the constant matrix which depends only on the even/odd parity of
n
via
\mu_{n}
,
e_{1}
,
e_{0}^{2}
, and
e_{\infty}^{2}
:

\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&-\dfrac{e_{0}e_{2}e_{\infty}}{\mathrm{i}}\left(\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}\right)^{-1}\\
\dfrac{\mathrm{i}}{e_{0}e_{2}e_{\infty}}\sqrt{\dfrac{1-e_{0}^{2}e_{1}^{2}}{e_{\infty}^{2}-e_{1}^{2}}}&0\end{bmatrix}.

(7.53)

Then the following asymptotic formula holds uniformly for
|\lambda|=1
and
z
bounded:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{n,-}(\lambda,z/n)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{n,+}(\lambda,z/n)={\rho_{\infty}}\begin{bmatrix}J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\\
J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{R}_{n,-}(\lambda,z/n)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{n,+}(\lambda,z/n)=}{}\times\dfrac{1}{\rho_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})&I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\\
-I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})&-I_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\end{bmatrix}^{-1}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big),

as
n\to\infty
along even/odd subsequences.

Proposition
7.8
suggests defining the following limiting Riemann–Hilbert problem.

Riemann–Hilbert Problem 7.9

(limiting problem, even/odd subsequences of
n
)
.

Fix generic monodromy parameters
(e_{1},e_{2})
, and
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|{\operatorname{Arg}}(z)|<\pi
. Seek a
2\times 2
matrix function
\lambda\mapsto\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
with the following properties:

•

Analyticity:
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
is an analytic function of
\lambda
for
|\lambda|\neq 1
.

•

Jump condition:

\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
takes analytic boundary values on the unit circle from the interior and exterior, denoted
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{+}(\lambda,z)
and
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{-}(\lambda,z)
for
|\lambda|=1
respectively, and they are related by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{+}(\lambda,z)=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{-}(\lambda,z){\rho_{\infty}}\begin{bmatrix}J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\\
J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\hat{\mathbf{R}}^\mathrm{even/odd}_+(\lambda,z)=}{}\times\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{\rho_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})&I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\\
-I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})&-I_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\end{bmatrix}^{-1}.

(7.54)

•

Normalization:

\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\to\mathbb{I}
as
\lambda\to\infty
.

Note that the Bessel functions
J_{\nu}(\rho_{\infty})
and
I_{\nu}(\rho_{0})
appearing in the jump matrix in (
7.54
) are analytic on the unit circle
|\lambda|=1
except at the point
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\operatorname{Arg}(z)}
. However, from the identities

\left.J_{\nu}(\rho_{\infty})\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}0}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\nu}\left.J_{\nu}(\rho_{\infty})\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}0}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left.I_{\nu}(\rho_{0})\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}0}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\nu}\left.I_{\nu}(\rho_{0})\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}0}}

and the fact that the indices
\nu
in each column of the Bessel matrix factors in (
7.54
) differ by
2
, combined with the fact that
\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
is an off-diagonal matrix, one sees easily that

\begin{bmatrix}J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\\
J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})&I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\\
-I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})&-I_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\end{bmatrix}^{-1}

is continuous at
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathrm{c}}
and hence is an analytic function of
\lambda
on the unit circle. The scalar factor
\rho_{\infty}/\rho_{0}
is also analytic for
|\lambda|=1
, and therefore the jump matrix in (
7.54
) is an analytic function of
\lambda
when
|\lambda|=1
. At this stage, the existence of a matrix function
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
satisfying Riemann–Hilbert Problem
7.9
is not clear. However, it turns out that there exists a discrete set
\Sigma^{\mathrm{even/odd}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
such that for
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\Sigma^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
, such a matrix does exist and is in fact a meromorphic function of
z
, see Section
9.1
below.

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
(e_{1},e_{2})
be generic monodromy parameters and take
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\Sigma^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathop{\lim_{n\to\infty}}_{\text{$n$ even/odd}}u_{n}\big(n^{-1}z;m\big)={}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{8\mu_{n}^{2}}{z}\big[\hat{R}_{11}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(0,z)+\hat{R}_{21}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(0,z)

\displaystyle-\hat{R}_{12}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(0,z)+\hat{R}_{22}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(0,z)\big]^{-2}.

(7.55)

Proof.

Noting that
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
necessarily has unit determinant, we form the matrix quotient

\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{R}_{n}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)^{-1},\qquad|\lambda|\neq 1.

Clearly,
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)
is analytic as a function of
\lambda
in the domain of definition, and for each fixed
n
it tends to
\mathbb{I}
as
\lambda\to\infty
as this is true for both
\mathbf{R}_{n}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)
and
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
. Across the unit circle, the boundary values of
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)
are related by

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{n,+}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{E}_{n,-}(\lambda,z)\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{-}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\big[\mathbf{R}_{n,-}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{n,+}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{E}_{n,+}(\lambda,z)=}{}\times\big[\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{-}(\lambda,z)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{+}(\lambda,z)\big]^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{-}(\lambda,z)^{-1},\qquad|\lambda|=1.

Thus, the jump matrix for
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)
is the conjugation, by a unit-determinant matrix function of
\lambda
independent of
n
, of the matrix ratio of the jump matrices for
\mathbf{R}_{n}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)
and for
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
. But by Proposition
7.8
, the latter ratio is
\mathbb{I}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)
uniformly on the unit circle as
n\to\infty
along even or odd subsequences. The conjugating factors exist and are uniformly bounded for
z
in compact subsets of
{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\Sigma(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
. It follows that in this limit,
\mathbf{E}_{n,+}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{E}_{n,-}(\lambda,z)\big(\mathbb{I}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-1}\big)\big)
uniformly for
|\lambda|=1
and
z
in compact subsets of
{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\Sigma(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
as
n\to\infty
. By standard small-norm theory,
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)
exists for large enough even or odd
n
, and tends to the identity as
n\to\infty
, in particular in the sense that

\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda(\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)-\mathbb{I})\to\mathbf{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{E}_{n}(0,z)\to\mathbb{I}

as
n\to\infty
along even/odd subsequences. By the definition of
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\lambda,z)
it follows that in the same limit

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda\big(\mathbf{R}_{n}\big(\lambda,n^{-1}z\big)-\mathbb{I}\big)\to\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda\big(\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)-\mathbb{I}\big),\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{n}\big(0,n^{-1}z\big)\to\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(0,z).

Combining (
6.6
) with (
7.43
) then shows (
7.55
).
Partly, this works because the dominant term in
\Xi_{n,12}^{(6)}\big(n^{-1}z;m\big)
is
A_{n,12}\big(n^{-1}z\big)
.
∎

7.6
Transformations of the limiting Riemann–Hilbert problem

In this section we transform Riemann–Hilbert Problem
7.9
to match the form of Riemann–Hilbert Problem
5.1
. To this end, using
[
38
, equations (10.4.4) and (10.4.6)]
to express the Bessel function
J_{\nu}(\diamond)
in terms of the Hankel functions
H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\diamond)
,
H_{\nu}^{(2)}(\diamond)
and the relations
[
38
, equation (10.4.4) and (10.4.6)]
,

\displaystyle H_{-\nu}^{(1)}(\diamond)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\nu}H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\diamond),\qquad H_{-\nu}^{(2)}(\diamond)=\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\nu}H_{\nu}^{(2)}(\diamond),

we arrive at the identity:

\displaystyle\rho_{\infty}\begin{bmatrix}J_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\\
J_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&-J_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\dfrac{\rho_{\infty}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H_{2\mu_{n}-1}^{(1)}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\\
H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{-1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-1\end{bmatrix}.

(7.56)

To obtain appropriate asymptotic formulæ for the matrix on the right-hand side of (
7.56
), we first apply the identity
[
38
, equation (10.6.1)]

H^{(k)}_{\nu-1}(\diamond)+H^{(k)}_{\nu+1}(\diamond)=\dfrac{2\nu}{\diamond}H^{(k)}_{\nu}(\diamond),\qquad k=1,2,

which gives

\displaystyle\rho_{\infty}^{-\sigma_{3}/2}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}\dfrac{\rho_{\infty}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H_{2\mu_{n}-1}^{(1)}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{-1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\dfrac{\sqrt{\rho_{\infty}}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H_{2\mu_{n}-1}^{(1)}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
4\mu_{n}H_{2\mu_{n}}^{(1)}(\rho_{\infty})&-4\mu_{n}H_{-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}.

(7.57)

The matrix on the right-hand side is amenable to asymptotic analysis as
\rho_{\infty}\to\infty
; using the asymptotics of Hankel functions
[
38
, equations (10.17.5) and (10.17.6)]
and (
7.57
) yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho_{\infty}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H_{2\mu_{n}-1}^{(1)}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{\infty})&H_{-1-2\mu_{n}}^{(2)}(\rho_{\infty})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}1&\dfrac{1}{2}-\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}-\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-1&\dfrac{1}{2}+\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}+\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}\Biggl(\mathbb{I}+\dfrac{1}{128\rho_{\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad=}{}\times\begin{bmatrix}\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)&\dfrac{\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-13\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)}{48\mu_{n}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
64\mu_{n}\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)&-\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(\rho_{\infty}^{-4}\big)\Biggr)\rho_{\infty}^{\sigma_{3}/2}

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad=}{}\times(2\sqrt{\mu_{n}})^{\mathbb{I}-\sigma_{3}}{\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}{}{}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\sigma_{3}/4}\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{i}\\
1&-\mathrm{i}\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\rho_{\infty}\sigma_{3}},\qquad\operatorname{Arg}(\rho_{\infty})\in(-\pi,\pi).

(7.58)

We turn to analogously treating the final factor of the jump of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)
; using
[
38
, equation (10.27.7)]
and the above relations, we have

\displaystyle{\rho_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}I_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\rho_{0})&I_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-I_{2\mu_{n}+1}(\rho_{0})&-I_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\rho_{0})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}-1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)&H^{(2)}_{1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
H^{(1)}_{1+2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)&H^{(2)}_{-1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{3\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}.

This allows us to find the following large-
\rho_{0}
asymptotics:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}-1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)&H^{(2)}_{1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}+1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)&H^{(2)}_{-1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}1&\dfrac{1}{2}-\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}-\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-1&\dfrac{1}{2}+\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}+\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}\Biggl(\mathbb{I}-\dfrac{1}{128\rho_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad=}{}\times\begin{bmatrix}\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)&\dfrac{\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-13\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)}{48\mu_{n}}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
64\mu_{n}\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)&-\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-9\big)\big(16\mu_{n}^{2}-1\big)\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(\rho_{0}^{-4}\big)\Biggr)\rho_{0}^{\sigma_{3}/2}

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad=}{}\times\big(2\sqrt{\mu_{n}}\big)^{\mathbb{I}-\sigma_{3}}{\dfrac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}{}{}\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{i}\\
1&-\mathrm{i}\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\rho_{0}\sigma_{3}},\qquad\operatorname{Arg}(\rho_{0})\in\left(-\dfrac{\pi}{2},\dfrac{3\pi}{2}\right).

(7.59)

For convenience, we introduce the notation

\mathbf{H}_{n}(\diamond)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sqrt{\dfrac{\pi}{4\mu_{n}}}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}/4}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\cdot\dfrac{\diamond}{2}\begin{bmatrix}H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}-1}(\diamond)&H^{(2)}_{1-2\mu_{n}}(\diamond)\vskip 2.84526pt\\
H^{(1)}_{1+2\mu_{n}}(\diamond)&H^{(2)}_{-1-2\mu_{n}}(\diamond)\end{bmatrix},

(7.60)

with a fixed determination of the square root; this choice of prefactor guarantees that we have
\det(\mathbf{H}_{n})=1
identically. Using the identity
[
38
, equation (10.11.4)]
, we note that
\mathbf{H}_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{n}(\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}}\diamond)=\mathbf{H}_{n}(\diamond)\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&2\cos(2\pi\mu_{n})\end{bmatrix}.

(7.61)

We can now rewrite the jump condition (
7.54
) as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{+}(\lambda,z)=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{-}(\lambda,z)\mathbf{H}_{n}(\rho_{\infty})\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-1\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\hat{\mathbf{R}}^\mathrm{even/odd}_+(\lambda,z)=}{}\times\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{3\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{-1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big).

Next, define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\dfrac{1}{2}-\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}-\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\vskip 4.2679pt\\
-1&\dfrac{1}{2}+\dfrac{\mu_{n}}{2}+\dfrac{3}{32\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\big(2\sqrt{\mu_{n}}\big)^{\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{\Omega}^\mathrm{even/odd} (\lambda, z) :=}{}\times\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\begin{cases}\mathbf{H}_{n}(\rho_{\infty}),&|\lambda|>1,\\
\mathbf{H}_{n}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big),&|\lambda|<1.\end{cases}

(7.62)

Then,
\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{+}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{\Omega}_{-}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-1\end{bmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{\Omega}^\mathrm{even/odd}_+(\lambda, z) =}{}\times\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{3\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}^{-1},\qquad|\lambda|=1,

(7.63)

where the jump depends only on the parity of
n
. Furthermore, since
\rho_{\infty}
and
\rho_{0}
change signs across the negative imaginary axis, we may use (
7.61
) to find

\mathbf{\Omega}_{+}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{\Omega}_{-}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&2\cos(2\pi\mu_{n})\end{bmatrix},

(7.64)

for
\lambda
on the negative imaginary axis with
|\lambda|>1
, oriented towards the origin and

\mathbf{\Omega}_{+}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{\Omega}_{-}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&2\cos(2\pi\mu_{n})\end{bmatrix},

(7.65)

for
\lambda
on the negative imaginary axis with
|\lambda|<1
, oriented away from the origin.

It follows from Riemann–Hilbert Problem
7.9
, (
7.62
), and (
7.58
) that
\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
has the following asymptotic behavior as
\lambda\to\infty
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)=\big(\mathbb{I}+\mathbf{\Xi^{\mathrm{even/odd}}}(z)\lambda^{-1}+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-2}\big)\big)\rho_{\infty}^{\sigma_{3}/2}{\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{i}&-1\\
1&-\mathrm{i}\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\rho_{\infty}\sigma_{3}},

(7.66)

where the
\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{-2}\big)
represents an asymptotic series that is differentiable term-by-term with respect to both
\lambda
and
z
. Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)=\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(z)\big(\mathbb{I}+\mathbf{\Pi}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(z)\lambda+\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\big)\rho_{0}^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\sigma_{3}/4}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(\lambda,z)=}{}\times{\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}{}{}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{i}&-1\\
1&-\mathrm{i}\end{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\rho_{0}\sigma_{3}},\qquad\lambda\to 0,

(7.67)

where
\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)
represents an asymptotic series at the origin
\lambda=0
which is similarly term-by-term differentiable.
Notice that we can now relate the limiting formula from Lemma
7.10
to
\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
using definitions (
7.62
) and (
7.60
) to find that

\displaystyle\hat{R}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{11}(0,z)+\hat{R}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{21}(0,z)-\hat{R}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{12}(0,z)-\hat{R}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{22}(0,z)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{4\mu_{n}}}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}/4}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}}{2}\big[\big(H^{(2)}_{-1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)+H^{(2)}_{1-2\mu_{n}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\big)\Omega^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{21}(\lambda,z)

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad=}{}-\big(H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}-1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)+H^{(1)}_{2\mu_{n}+1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/2}\rho_{0}\big)\big)\Omega^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{22}(\lambda,z)\big]_{\lambda=0}.

Then, using (
7.67
) and (
7.59
) yields

\displaystyle\mathop{\lim_{n\to\infty}}_{\text{$n$ even/odd}}u_{n}\big(n^{-1}z\big)=U^{\mathrm{even/odd}}(z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=-\frac{1}{2z\Delta^{\mathrm{even/odd}}_{21}(z)^{2}}.

To extract the monodromy parameters of
U(z)
from
\mathbf{\Omega}(\lambda,z)
, we notice that it solves Riemann–Hilbert Problem
5.1
with

t_{1}^{\infty}=t_{0}^{0}=-2\cos(2\pi\mu_{n})=-\left(e_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{e_{1}^{2}}\right),

(7.68)

and

\mathbf{C}_{0\infty}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-1\end{bmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\\
-\mathrm{e}^{3\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}&-\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}.

(7.69)

Since
\mu_{n}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{even/odd}}
depend only on the parity of
n
(see (
6.8
), (
7.53
), respectively), and
{e_{1}=e_{1,n}=\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}}}
(see Remark
6.3
), it follows that (
7.68
)–(
7.69
) depend only on the parity of
n
. Recalling the formulæ for
y_{i}
in Section
5.4
, one immediately arrives at formulæ (
1.20
)–(
1.22
).

8
Small
\boldsymbol{x}
asymptotics and proof of Proposition
1.5

Inspired by
[
33
]
, see also
[
22
, Theorem 3.2]
, the goal of this section is to compute the asymptotics as
x\to 0
of the Bäcklund iterates
u_{n}(x)
for fixed
n
and, by evaluating at
n=0
, arriving at the asymptotic behavior of a generic solution of PIII(
D_{6}
) in this limit. Observe that the matrices
\mathbf{\Xi}^{(6)}_{n}(x)
and
\mathbf{\Delta}^{(6)}_{n}(x)
defined in (
6.5
) and (
6.4
), respectively, can be expressed in terms of
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
as follows:

\mathbf{\Xi}^{(6)}_{n}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{n}(x)+\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda(\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)-\mathbb{I})-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}x\sigma_{3},\qquad\mathbf{\Delta}^{(6)}_{n}(x)=\mathbf{Q}_{n}(0,x).

Using (
6.6
) then gives

u_{n}(x)=\frac{\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}A_{n,12}(x)-\mathrm{i}\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda Q_{n,12}(\lambda,x)}{Q_{n,11}(0,x)Q_{n,12}(0,x)}.

(8.1)

Also, combining (
7.18
) and (
7.21
), we have

A_{n,12}(x)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\mu_{n}\big(1-a_{n}^{2}\big)}{\gamma_{n}x^{2-2\kappa_{n}}}

(8.2)

in which all
x
-dependence is explicit. To analyze
u_{n}(x)
for small
x
it therefore remains to obtain asymptotics of
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\lambda,x)
as
x\to 0
.
To do this, let
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}
denote the jump matrix (
7.40
). Then, denoting the off-diagonal constant matrix

\mathbf{O}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{J}_{n}e_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\beta_{n}\\
\beta_{n}^{-1}&0\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}_{-n}^{-1},

(8.3)

we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\lambda,x)=\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})\mathbf{O}_{n}\mathbf{M}\bigl(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1};\kappa_{-n},\mu_{n}\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{V}_\mathbf{Q}(\lambda,x)=}{}\times\mathbf{D}_{-n}^{-1}x^{-\kappa_{-n}\sigma_{3}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{-\sigma_{3}/2}e_{0}^{-3\sigma_{3}},\qquad|\lambda|=1,

where matrices
\mathbf{D}_{n}
,
\mathbf{M}
are defined in (
7.24
) and (
7.31
), respectively. To consider the limit
x\to 0
, we start with the Whittaker matrix
\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})
. Using
[
38
, equation (13.14.6)]
, we obtain

M_{\kappa,\mu}(z)=z^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}\left(1-\frac{\kappa}{1+2\mu}z+\mathcal{O}\big(z^{2}\big)\right),\qquad z\to 0.

Therefore, using the definition (
7.31
) of
\mathbf{M}(Z;\kappa,\mu)
we have

\displaystyle(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{n}&\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{n}\\[5.69046pt]
1&1\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda x)=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{M}_{\infty}(\lambda x),

in the limit
x\to 0
uniformly for
|\lambda|=1
. Similarly, in the same limit,

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\mathbf{M}\bigl(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1};\kappa_{-n},\mu_{-n}\bigr)\big(\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{-\mu_{-n}\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{-n}&\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{-n}\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}\big(x\lambda^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{M}_{0}\big(x\lambda^{-1}\big)=\mathbf{M}_{0}(0)+\mathcal{O}\big(x\lambda^{-1}\big).

For
x>0
, the functions
\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{p}
,
\bigl(-\lambda^{-1}\bigr)_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{p}
,
(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{p}
, and
\big(\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{p}
(the latter two being principal branches) all have the same branch cut, namely
\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}_{-}
. One has the following identities:

(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda)^{p}=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi p/2}x^{p}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{p},\qquad\big(\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{p}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi p/2}x^{p}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{-p},\qquad\big(-\lambda^{-1}\big)^{p}_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi p}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{-p}.

It follows that

\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\sigma_{3}/2}x^{\kappa_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{M}(\mathrm{i}x\lambda;\kappa_{n},\mu_{n})=x^{(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{M}_{\infty}(\lambda x)x^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}/2}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}},

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}\bigl(-\mathrm{i}x\lambda^{-1};\kappa_{-n},\mu_{-n}\bigr)^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{-n}^{-1}x^{-\kappa_{-n}\sigma_{3}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{-\sigma_{3}/2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\mu_{-n}\sigma_{3}/2}x^{-\mu_{-n}\sigma_{3}}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\mu_{-n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{M}_{0}\big(x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}\mathbf{D}_{-n}^{-1}x^{(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n})\sigma_{3}}.

Because
\mathbf{O}_{n}
is off-diagonal, the central factors in
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\lambda,x)
simplify as follows:

x^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}\pi\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{O}_{n}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}\pi\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}x^{-\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\lambda_{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{Figures/squarell.pdf}}}}^{\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}=\mathbf{O}_{n}x^{-2\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\lambda,x)=x^{(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}\mathbf{D}_{n}{}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{\infty}(\lambda x)\cdot\mathbf{O}_{n}x^{-2\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{V}_\mathbf{Q}(\lambda,x)=}{}\times\mathbf{M}_{0}\big(x\lambda^{-1}\big)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}/4}\mathbf{D}_{-n}^{-1}x^{(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n})\sigma_{3}}e_{0}^{-3\sigma_{3}}.

(8.4)

The matrix
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\lambda,x)
does not possess a finite limit as
x\to 0
due to the factors
x^{(\kappa_{n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}
,
x^{(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{-n})\sigma_{3}}
; this can be handled by introducing the following transformation. Let
\varsigma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lambda x
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{n}(\varsigma,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}}x^{(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n})\sigma_{3}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\begin{cases}\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\frac{\varsigma}{x},x)x^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\kappa_{n})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{n},&|\varsigma|>1,\\
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\frac{\varsigma}{x},x)\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\frac{\varsigma}{x},x)e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}x^{(\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{-n}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}}\\
\quad\times\mathbf{M}_{0}(0)x^{2\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{O}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{\infty}(\varsigma)^{-1},&|x|<|\varsigma|<1,\\
\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\frac{\varsigma}{x},x)e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}x^{(\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{D}_{-n}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{i}\pi\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{M}_{0}(0)x^{2\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{O}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{\infty}(\varsigma)^{-1},&|\varsigma|<|x|.\end{cases}

(8.5)

It follows that
\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{n}
is analytic as a function of
\varsigma
for
\varsigma\in{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{|\varsigma|=1\}
and satisfies

\lim_{\varsigma\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{n}(\varsigma,x)=\mathbb{I}.

Furthermore, on the circle
|\varsigma|=1
, the jump condition
\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{n,+}(\varsigma,x)=\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{n,-}(\varsigma,x)\mathbf{V}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}}(\varsigma,x)
holds, where the jump contour has counterclockwise orientation, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}}(\varsigma,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\sigma_{3}/4}x^{(\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{n})\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\frac{\varsigma}{x},x\right)e_{0}^{3\sigma_{3}}x^{(\kappa_{-n}-\frac{1}{2})\sigma_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{\pi\mathrm{i}\sigma_{3}/4}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbf{V}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}}}(\varsigma,x) :=}{}\times\mathbf{D}_{-n}\mathbf{M}_{0}(0)x^{2\mu_{n}\sigma_{3}}\mathbf{O}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{-1}(\varsigma).

Using (
8.4
) immediately yields
